<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Let <i>X</i> be a smooth projective complex variety with smoothly semipositive anticanonical bundle. For any torus linearization of that bundle, we show that invariant sections in unbounded degrees with one fixed negative pseudoeffective error yield an invariant section in a positive untwisted degree. Combining the natural invariant index with compact-monodromy structure, we deduce that every such <i>X</i> has a nonzero section of some positive anticanonical power.

- **claim-invariant-fixed-error-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inj-transfer)): Let $X$ be smooth connected projective, let $L=-K_X$ carry a smooth semipositive metric, and let an algebraic torus $T$ act on $X$. Let $P,M$ be linearized line bundles such that $P=L$ as ordinary line bundles and $-M$ is pseudoeffective. The linearization of $P$ may differ from the natural one on $L$. Suppose that for an unbounded $I\subset\mathbb Z_{>0}$ there are nonzero invariant sections $$s_i\in H^0(X,M+iP)^T\qquad(i\in I).$$ Then $H^0(X,mP)^T\ne0$ for some integer $m>0$.
  - Explanation: An unbounded family of invariant sections with a fixed negative pseudoeffective error yields an untwisted invariant anticanonical section while preserving the chosen linearization.
- **claim-ordinary-anticanonical-nonvanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-ordinary)): For every smooth connected projective complex $X$ with smoothly semipositive $-K_X$, some positive multiple $-mK_X$ has a nonzero section.
  - Explanation: Smooth anticanonical semipositivity on a smooth connected projective complex variety guarantees a nonzero section in some positive anticanonical degree.
- **claim-equidimensional-models** (construction, [source](#stmt-val-flat-model)): Let $X\dashrightarrow Y$ be a dominant rational map of integral projective varieties over $\mathbb C$. There are a smooth projective birational model $Y'$ of $Y$ and an integral projective flat family $X_0\to Y'$ with a birational morphism to the graph of the original map. Every irreducible component of every fiber has dimension $\dim X-\dim Y$. The normalization $V$ of $X_0$ is equidimensional over $Y'$; in particular, every vertical prime divisor of $V$ maps onto a prime divisor of $Y'$. If $\mathbb C(Y)$ is algebraically closed in $\mathbb C(X)$, the morphism from $V$, and from any smooth resolution of $V$, to $Y'$ has connected fibers. If a torus acts on $X$ and the rational map is invariant, these constructions and a projective resolution of $V$ can be made equivariant, with trivial action on the base.
  - Explanation: A dominant projective rational map admits a flat integral model and an equidimensional normalization. The additional function-field condition gives connected fibers, and invariant torus actions admit equivariant models.
- **claim-divisorial-maximum-metric** (construction, [source](#stmt-har-prop-maximum)): Let $X,Y$ be smooth connected projective, let $L=-K_X$ have a smooth semipositive metric, and suppose $$V\xrightarrow{\nu}W\xrightarrow{\mu}X,\qquad g:W\to Y, \qquad f=g\nu,$$ where $W$ is normal, $g$ is projective surjective equidimensional, $\mu$ is birational, and $V$ is a smooth projective resolution. Set $D_V=(\mu\nu)^*L$ and $E=K_{V/X}$, and let $s_E$ be the canonical Jacobian section of $E$. Suppose a nonzero rational section $s$ of $b\mu^*L$, $b>0$, has no horizontal poles and, on the generic fiber $V_\eta$, satisfies $$H^0(V_\eta,E+kbD_V)=\mathbb C(Y)\,s_Es^k \qquad(k\ge0).$$ Alternatively, suppose a torus acts compatibly on the diagram, trivially on $Y$, and let $P$ be $L$ with any chosen linearization. Put $P_V=(\mu\nu)^*P$. In this case it suffices that $s$ be an invariant rational section of $b\mu^*P$ and that the displayed condition be replaced by $$H^0(V_\eta,E+kbP_V)^T=\mathbb C(Y)\,s_Es^k \qquad(k\ge0),$$ where $E$ carries its natural Jacobian action.* *After replacing $b,s$ by a multiple and a power, there are a line $Q$ on $Y$ and a regular injection $$g^*Q\longrightarrow b\mu^*L$$ whose squared fiber-maximum norms define a positive continuous metric with plurisubharmonic weights on $Q$. In the invariant case $Q$ has trivial linearization and the injection $g^*Q\to b\mu^*P$ is equivariant.
  - Explanation: Divisorial normalization of a relative rational section produces a regular injection from a base line. Generic adjoint rigidity makes its fiberwise maximum a positive continuous semipositive metric, with an equivariant version for the specified character.
- **claim-invariant-anticanonical-index** (theorem, [source](#stmt-har-prop-index)): Let $F$ be a compact complex manifold with a holomorphic action of a compact real torus $T$. Give $D=-K_F$ its natural linearization. There are an integer $a>0$ and a polynomial $P\in\mathbb Q[t]$ such that $$\sum_q(-1)^q\dim H^q(F,maD)^T=P(m)\quad(m>0),\qquad P(0)=\sum_q(-1)^q\dim H^q(F,\mathcal O_F)^T.$$ The statement includes the trivial torus, an empty fixed locus, and zero-dimensional fixed components; it assumes no positivity, projectivity, or Kähler condition.
  - Explanation: The naturally linearized invariant anticanonical index agrees with a rational polynomial along an arithmetic progression, and its constant term is the actual invariant structure-sheaf index.
- **claim-invariant-forms-and-determinants** (construction, [source](#stmt-har-lem-forms)): Let $X$ be smooth connected projective, let $L=-K_X$ have a smooth semipositive metric, and let an algebraic torus $T$ act on $X$. If $\chi^T(X,\mathcal O_X)\ne0$, then for some fixed $q$ there are nonzero invariant sections of $\Omega_X^{n-q}\otimes L^{m+1}$ for unbounded positive integers $m$. If these do not already give a nonzero invariant section of a positive power of $L$, there is an integer $e>0$, a linearized line bundle $M$, a nonzero map $M\to\bigwedge^e\Omega_X^{n-q}$, and invariant sections $$0\ne s_i\in H^0(X,M+a_iL)^T,\qquad a_i\longrightarrow\infty.$$
  - Explanation: A nonzero invariant Euler characteristic supplies invariant twisted forms in a fixed differential degree. Unless these already yield anticanonical sections, determinant extraction gives an unbounded fixed-error family.
- **claim-natural-invariant-nonvanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $X$ be smooth connected projective over $\mathbb C$, with smoothly semipositive $L=-K_X$. If an algebraic torus $T$ acts on $X$ and $\chi(X,\mathcal O_X)\ne0$, then $H^0(X,mL)^T\ne0$ for some integer $m>0$, with the natural linearization. The torus may be trivial.
  - Explanation: A nonzero structure-sheaf Euler characteristic gives natural torus-invariant anticanonical nonvanishing under smooth semipositivity.
- **claim-curvature-null-determinant-divisors** (bound, [source](#stmt-har-lem-null-divisors)): Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective variety of dimension $n$, and let $L=-K_X$ have smooth semipositive curvature $\theta$ of maximal rank $r<n$. Suppose a line bundle $M$ admits a nonzero map $M\to\bigwedge^e\Omega_X^p$ for integers $e,p>0$. Then $c_1(M)G^{n-1}\le0$ for every Kähler class $G$. If $0\ne s\in H^0(X,M+aL)$ for an integer $a$, its zero divisor $D$ satisfies $$[D][\theta]^r[\omega]^{n-r-1}=0$$ for every Kähler form $\omega$. Each component has zero intersection separately, and $\theta^r$ vanishes on its smooth locus.
  - Explanation: A determinant line injected into differential forms has nonpositive Kähler degree. Effective divisors of its anticanonical twists have vanishing intersection with the maximal curvature power, component by component.
- **claim-source-character-bound-and-index** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-source-character)): Let a torus $T$ act on a smooth connected projective $X$ with a linearized ample line. Let $U$ be a $T$-linearized line, let $\lambda$ be a generic integral cocharacter with $X^\lambda=X^T$, and let $S$ be its source. Write $\mu$ for the full $T$-character of $U\|_S$. Every nonzero eigensection of $U$ has $\lambda$-weight at most $\langle\mu,\lambda\rangle$. If $U$ is nef, every fixed-component fiber weight has pairing at most that value and, for every $m\ge0$, $$\label{inj:extreme-index} [\chi_T(X,mU)]_{m\mu}=\chi(S,mU\|_S).$$ The trivial torus is allowed, with $S=X$ and $\mu=0$.
  - Explanation: The source fiber character bounds eigensection weights. For a nef line, the extreme full-character coefficient of the equivariant index is the Euler characteristic on the source.
- **claim-exact-source-character-attainment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inj-extremal-theorem)): Let $X$ be smooth connected projective with smoothly semipositive $L=-K_X$ and with $H^0(X,\Omega_X^p)=0$ for all $p>0$. Let an algebraic torus $T$ act with a linearized ample bundle. For every generic integral cocharacter $\lambda$ with $X^\lambda=X^T$, let $S$ be its source and let $\mu$ be the full $T$-character of $L\|_S$. Then for some $k_\mu>0$ there is a nonzero section of $k_\mu L$ of character $k_\mu\mu$. In particular a positive power of $L$ has a nonzero invariant section, for the natural linearization.
  - Explanation: When positive-degree holomorphic forms vanish, each generic source character is attained by an anticanonical eigensection. Products of attained characters then yield a natural invariant section.
- **claim-injectivity-base-cohomology** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inj-leray)): Let $g:V\to Y$ be a morphism of smooth projective complex varieties, let $A$ be very ample on $Y$, and give $A$ a smooth metric with positive curvature $\omega_A$. Suppose a line bundle $N$ on $V$ has a singular Hermitian metric $h$ such that $$\mathcal I(h)=\mathcal O_V, \qquad i\Theta_h(N)\ge\epsilon g^*\omega_A \quad\text{for some }\epsilon>0.$$ Then $$H^i(Y,R^jg_*\mathcal O_V(K_V+N))=0 \qquad(i>0,\ j\ge0).$$ Disconnected smooth source varieties are allowed, with the hypotheses on each component.
  - Explanation: A singular coefficient metric with trivial multiplier ideal and strict base-curvature domination forces positive base-degree cohomology of all adjoint higher direct images to vanish.
- **claim-ordinary-fixed-error-conversion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inj-ordinary-conversion)): Let $X$ be smooth connected projective over $\mathbb C$ and let $L=-K_X$ carry a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric. Suppose that $F_0$ is a pseudoeffective line bundle and, for an unbounded set $S\subset\mathbb Z_{>0}$, there are effective integral divisors $$N_s\sim sL-F_0\qquad(s\in S).$$ Then $H^0(X,mL)\ne0$ for some integer $m>0$.
  - Explanation: Unbounded effective anticanonical twists with a fixed pseudoeffective subtraction give an ordinary positive anticanonical section through an Iitaka-base conversion.
- **claim-twisted-form-conversion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inj-forms-input)): Under the smooth projective and smooth anticanonical semipositivity hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#inj:ordinary-conversion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inj:ordinary-conversion">13</a>, suppose $H^0(X,\Omega_X^p\otimes mL)\ne0$ for an unbounded set of positive integers $m$ and a fixed $p\ge0$. Then a positive power of $L$ has a nonzero section.
  - Explanation: Unbounded nonzero anticanonically twisted forms in a fixed differential degree imply ordinary anticanonical nonvanishing.
- **claim-uniform-semipositive-smoothing** (construction, [source](#stmt-har-lem-smoothing)): Let $Q$ be a line bundle on a smooth projective $Y$, with a positive continuous Hermitian metric whose local weights are plurisubharmonic. For a fixed Kähler form $\eta$, there are smooth metrics whose weights converge uniformly to these weights and whose curvatures are at least $-\varepsilon_j\eta$, with $\varepsilon_j\to0$.
  - Explanation: Continuous plurisubharmonic metric weights admit uniform smooth approximations with negative curvature errors tending to zero.
- **claim-kernel-condition-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-har-thm-kernel)): Let $f:V\to Y$ be a surjective morphism between smooth connected projective complex varieties. Let $D_V$ be a line bundle with a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric, whose curvature is $\theta$. Let $Q$ be a line bundle on $Y$ with a positive continuous Hermitian metric having plurisubharmonic weights. Assume that on a nonempty ordinary open subset of the submersion locus, $$\label{har:eq:kernel} \ker\theta\subseteq\ker df.$$ Then, for every $k\ge0$, $q\ge0$, and $p>0$, $$\label{har:eq:vanishing} H^p\bigl(Y,R^q f_*(K_V+D_V)\otimes Q^k\bigr)=0.$$ In applications below $D_V$ is the pullback of a smoothly semipositive anticanonical line, and the particular diagram supplies <a href="#har:eq:kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="har:eq:kernel">[har:eq:kernel]</a>; the rank-open condition is not inferred from nefness.
  - Explanation: A vertical curvature kernel on an ordinary open subset gives vanishing of positive base-degree adjoint cohomology for every nonnegative continuous semipositive base twist.
- **claim-pseudoeffective-domination-vanishing** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-har-prop-dominationvanish)): In Theorem <a href="#har:thm:kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="har:thm:kernel">16</a>, replace the kernel hypothesis by the assumption that $bD_V-f^*H$ is pseudoeffective for some $b>0$ and some ample line $H$ on $Y$. Then the same vanishing holds.
  - Explanation: Pseudoeffective domination of an ample base pullback provides an alternative to the curvature-kernel condition in the harmonic vanishing theorem.
- **claim-birational-euler-interpolation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-har-prop-euler)): Let $\pi:V\to X$ be birational between smooth connected projective varieties, let $L=-K_X$ be smoothly semipositive, put $D_V=\pi^*L$, and write $E=K_{V/X}=K_V+D_V$. Let $f:V\to Y$ be surjective, with $Y$ smooth connected projective. Suppose a continuously semipositive line $Q$ on $Y$ and an injection $f^*Q\to bD_V$, $b>0$, satisfy either vanishing criterion above. Then $H^0(X,kbL)\ne0$ for some $k>0$. If a torus acts, trivially on $Y$ and $Q$, and all maps and the injection are equivariant, this section can be taken invariant.
  - Explanation: A semipositive base line injecting into the anticanonical pullback, together with the stated vanishing criterion, yields a section by interpolation from the exceptional canonical section at zero. Equivariance preserves invariance.
- **claim-invariant-pole-sheaf-stabilization** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-har-prop-pole-stabilization)): Let $V\to W\to X$, $g:W\to Y$, $f:V\to Y$, and $D_V=\pi^*(-K_X)$ be the normal equidimensional model and smooth resolution in the preceding invariant Iitaka construction. Put $E=K_{V/X}$. Retain its invariant adjoint rank-one condition <a href="#har:eq:invrank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="har:eq:invrank">[har:eq:invrank]</a> and its curvature-kernel inclusion, and write $bD_V=G+f^*Q$ after vertical normalization, with $G$ pulled back from the normal model and omitting a divisorial lift of each base prime. Then the increasing invariant pole sheaves $\mathcal P_k=(f_*\mathcal O_V(E+kG))^T$, viewed as rational functions through $s_Es_G^k$, stabilize. The line $Q$ is nef and $G$ contains no entire fiber. These conclusions give a second proof that the fiberwise maximum defines a positive continuous metric.
  - Explanation: On the specified invariant Iitaka model, bounded divisorial poles force the increasing pole sheaves to stabilize. Harmonic vanishing gives nefness, and a blow-up contradiction excludes a wholly vanishing fiber.
- **claim-source-retraction-line-map** (construction, [source](#stmt-har-lem-source)): Let $S$ be smooth connected projective with a $\mathbb C^*$-action, allowing the trivial action. Let $S_0$ be its attracting component for $t\to0$, and write $w_C(U)$ for the fiber weight of a linearized line $U$ on a fixed component $C$. A nonzero eigensection of $U$ has weight at most $w_{S_0}(U)$. If $U$ is nef, then $w_C(U)\le w_{S_0}(U)$ for every fixed component, and the multiplicity of weight $mw_{S_0}(U)$ in the virtual character $\chi(S,mU)$ is $\chi(S_0,mU\|_{S_0})$, for $m>0$. If $U$ is nef, on a smooth equivariant resolution $d:\widehat S\to S$ of the source retraction $p:\widehat S\to S_0$, there is an equivariant nonzero bundle map $p^*(U\|_{S_0})\to d^*U$.
  - Explanation: The attracting source bounds section and fixed-fiber weights. For a nef line, resolving the source retraction also gives a regular equivariant map from its source restriction.
- **claim-rationally-connected-source-weight** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-har-prop-extremal)): Let $S$ be smooth connected projective rationally connected, with smoothly semipositive $L=-K_S$, and fix a $\mathbb C^*$-action with the natural anticanonical linearization. If $w=w_{S_0}(L)$, then some $\ell>0$ has a nonzero section of $\ell L$ of weight $\ell w$.
  - Explanation: For a rationally connected variety, the natural source weight is attained exactly by a section of a positive anticanonical power.
- **claim-rationally-connected-torus-invariants** (theorem, [source](#stmt-har-cor-torus)): Every algebraic torus acting on the rationally connected $S$ of Proposition <a href="#har:prop:extremal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="har:prop:extremal">22</a> has a nonzero invariant section of some positive power of $-K_S$.
  - Explanation: Extremal character sections on a rationally connected variety can be multiplied to produce a natural invariant anticanonical section for an entire algebraic torus.
- **claim-ordinary-cohomology-alteration-conversion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-alteration-conversion)): Let $X$ be smooth connected projective, with smoothly semipositive $L=-K_X$. Suppose that for one fixed $q\ge0$, $H^q(X,mL)\ne0$ for unbounded positive integers $m$. Then $H^0(X,rL)\ne0$ for some $r>0$.
  - Explanation: Ordinary cohomology nonvanishing in a fixed degree along unbounded anticanonical powers implies section nonvanishing. The geometric route descends a metrized line and its injection from a reduced flat alteration.
- **claim-localized-multiplier-ideal-vanishing** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inj-local-nadel)): Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety of dimension $d>0$. Suppose a line bundle $R$ has a singular Hermitian metric with plurisubharmonic weights $\psi$. Assume that on some coordinate ball these weights are continuous and their curvature dominates a positive smooth $(1,1)$-form. Then $$H^q\bigl(Y,\mathcal O_Y(K_Y+R)\otimes\mathcal I(\psi)\bigr)=0 \qquad(q>0).$$
  - Explanation: Global plurisubharmonic weights that are continuous and strictly positive on a coordinate ball yield global adjoint vanishing with their multiplier ideal.
- **claim-horizontal-direct-image-strictness** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inj-strict-direct-image)): Let $p:V\to Y$ be a proper holomorphic submersion with compact fibers and Kähler total space. Let $D$ have a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric. Work on an open set where $p_*(K_{V/Y}+D)$ is locally free and its fibers are the corresponding section spaces. Suppose the coefficient curvature dominates the pullback of a positive base form on an open patch meeting $V_{y_0}$, and let $u_0\in H^0(V_{y_0},K_{V_{y_0}}+D\|_{V_{y_0}})$ be a section that is nonzero on that patch. The curvature of the direct-image metric paired with $u_0$ at $y_0$ is strictly positive in every nonzero tangent direction of $Y$. If a compact torus acts over the trivial base action and preserves the metrics, let the entire invariant summand, or an entire character summand, have rank one and suppose $u_0$ belongs to that summand. Its quotient metric under the holomorphic orthogonal projection has the same strictness at $y_0$.
  - Explanation: Horizontal curvature domination where a fiber adjoint section is nonzero makes its direct-image curvature pairing strictly positive. An entire rank-one torus character summand inherits this through its orthogonal quotient.
- **claim-exact-invariant-adjoint-ideal** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inj-exact-adjoint-ideal)): Let $X,V,Y$ be smooth connected projective varieties with $\dim Y>0$, let $\pi:V\to X$ be birational, and let $f:V\to Y$ be surjective. Suppose an algebraic torus $T$ acts equivariantly on these maps and trivially on $Y$. Give $L=-K_X$ its natural linearization and a smooth semipositive metric invariant under the maximal compact torus. Put $L'=\pi^*L$, $J=K_{V/X}$, and let $t$ be its natural invariant Jacobian section. Assume that $$\mathcal B=(f_*\mathcal O_V(J))^T$$ has rank one. Via division by $t$, view $\mathcal B$ as a sheaf of rational functions on $Y$, and write $\mathcal B^{\vee\vee}=\mathcal O_Y(B_0)$ and $R=B_0-K_Y$. Then the invariant adjoint $L^2$ metric extends to a singular semipositive metric $\psi_R$ on $R$, and $$\label{inj:exact-ideal} \mathcal O_Y(B_0)\otimes\mathcal I(\psi_R)=\mathcal B.$$ If, on a nonempty patch in the smooth base-change locus where $t$ is nonzero, the curvature of $L'$ dominates the pullback of a positive base form, then $\psi_R$ is smooth and strictly positive on a base coordinate ball.
  - Explanation: The multiplier ideal of the extended invariant adjoint metric recovers the actual rank-one direct image inside its reflexive hull, including its defect in higher codimension. Horizontal strictness gives a strictly positive base ball.
- **claim-degree-zero-field-invariant-sections** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inj-degree-zero-route)): Let $X$ be smooth connected projective with $H^q(X,\mathcal O_X)=0$ for $q>0$. Suppose $L=-K_X$ has a smooth semipositive metric and an algebraic torus $T$ acts on $X$. Then $H^0(X,mL)^T\ne0$ for some $m>0$, with the natural anticanonical linearization.
  - Explanation: Vanishing of positive structure-sheaf cohomology enables a natural invariant section construction using the null-pole field and the exact degree-zero direct-image ideal.
- **claim-stabilized-order-invariant-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-inj-stabilized-transfer)): Let $X$ be smooth connected projective and let an algebraic torus $T$ act on $X$. Give $L=-K_X$ its natural linearization and suppose it admits a smooth semipositive metric. Let $M$ be a linearized line bundle with $-M$ pseudoeffective. If there are nonzero sections $\sigma_j\in H^0(X,M+jL)^T$ for an unbounded set $J\subset\mathbb Z_{>0}$, then $H^0(X,mL)^T\ne0$ for some integer $m>0$.
  - Explanation: For the natural linearization, unbounded invariant fixed-error sections transfer to untwisted sections by retaining actual direct-image lines and stabilizing their boundary orders.
- **claim-covariant-tensor-holonomy-dichotomy** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-tensor-holonomy)): Let $P$ be a compact simply connected nonflat Kähler factor, irreducible in the Riemannian de Rham sense, with nonnegative Ricci curvature. Either $K_P$ has a nonzero parallel holomorphic volume form, or $$H^0\bigl(P,(\Omega_P^1)^{\otimes m}\bigr)=0 \qquad\text{for every integer }m>0.$$
  - Explanation: An irreducible simply connected nonflat Kähler factor with nonnegative Ricci curvature either has a parallel canonical volume form or has no holomorphic covariant tensor of positive degree.

          "source_id": "PM-068.4"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-har-lem-forms",
        "exact_quote": "If $\\chi^T(X,\\mathcal O_X)\\ne0$, then for some fixed $q$ there are nonzero invariant sections of $\\Omega_X^{n-q}\\otimes L^{m+1}$ for unbounded positive integers $m$.         "source_id": "PM-068.4"
      },

Every nonzero eigensection of $U$ has $\\lambda$-weight at most $\\langle\\mu,\\lambda\\rangle$. If $U$ is nef, every fixed-component fiber weight has pairing at most that value and, for every $m\\ge0$, $$\\label{inj:extreme-index}\n [\\chi_T(X,mU)]_{m\\mu}=\\chi(S,mU|_S).$$ The trivial torus is allowed, with $S=X$ and $\\mu=0$.",

        "exact_quote": "Then, for every $k\\ge0$, $q\\ge0$, and $p>0$, $$\\label{har:eq:vanishing}\n H^p\\bigl(Y,R^q f_*(K_V+D_V)\\otimes Q^k\\bigr)=0.$$",

          "exact_quote": "Let $f:V\\to Y$ be a surjective morphism between smooth connected projective complex varieties.           "source_id": "PM-068.4"
        }
      ],

          "exact_quote": "Let $\\pi:V\\to X$ be birational between smooth connected projective varieties, let $L=-K_X$ be smoothly semipositive, put $D_V=\\pi^*L$, and write $E=K_{V/X}=K_V+D_V$.         "exact_quote": "A nonzero eigensection of $U$ has weight at most $w_{S_0}(U)$. If $U$ is nef, on a smooth equivariant resolution $d:\\widehat S\\to S$ of the source retraction $p:\\widehat S\\to S_0$, there is an equivariant nonzero bundle map $p^*(U|_{S_0})\\to d^*U$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Anticanonical line** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective variety over $\mathbb C$ and put $L=-K_X=\det T_X$.
- **Smooth anticanonical semipositivity** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We assume that $L$ is smoothly semipositive: it has a smooth Hermitian metric with nonnegative Chern curvature.
- **Torus linearization** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A $T$-linearization of a line bundle is a lift of this action to its total space, linear on each fiber.
- **Chosen anticanonical linearization** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We write $P$ for $L$ equipped with any such linearization, and use additive notation for tensor products.
- **Negative pseudoeffective error** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The assumption on the error is that $-M$ is pseudoeffective: its numerical class lies in the closure of the effective cone.
- **Equivalence notation** ([source](#pm-h-6)): We write $\sim$, $\sim_{\mathbb Q}$, and $\equiv$ for linear, rational linear, and numerical equivalence.
- **Continuous semipositive metric** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A continuous semipositive metric has continuous plurisubharmonic weights.
- **Locally bounded weights** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Locally bounded weights have both upper and lower bounds and need not be continuous.
- **Action on sections** ([source](#pm-h-6)): The action on sections is $(g\cdot s)(gx)=g(s(x))$.
- **Source component** ([source](#inj:extremal-weights)): A source component is the fixed component whose attracting set as $t\to0$ is open.
- **Curvature-null small divisor** ([source](#har:subsec:small)): Call a prime divisor small if $\theta^r$ vanishes on its smooth locus.
- **Invariant small-pole field** ([source](#har:subsec:small)): Let $\mathcal K$ consist of zero and the invariant rational functions with small pole support.
- **Curvature-null field** ([source](#har:subsec:kernelfield)): On the ordinary open $U$ of maximal curvature rank, define $$\mathcal K=\{g\in\mathbb C(X): dg(\ker\theta)=0 \text{ on }U\text{ off a proper algebraic subset}\}.$$
- **Degree-zero invariant pole field** ([source](#inj:localized)): Let $K$ be the field of invariant rational functions whose polar divisors have degree zero.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-positive-transfer-exponent | m | > | $0$ | Existential positive integer exponent; no uniform bound is supplied. |
| q-determinant-kahler-degree | c_1(M)G^{n-1} | ≤ | $0$ | Each Kähler class under the stated curvature and cotangent-inclusion hypotheses. |
| q-null-divisor-intersection | [D][\theta]^r[\omega]^{n-r-1} | = | $0$ | The maximal curvature rank is strictly below the dimension; the equality holds separately for each component. |
| q-invariant-transfer-adjoint-rank | \operatorname{rank}\bigl(f_*\mathcal O_V(E+jkP')\bigr)^T | = | $1$ | Every nonnegative twist, including the zero exponent. |
| q-ordinary-generic-adjoint-dimension | h^0\bigl(F,(E+kdL')\|_F\bigr) | = | $1$ | All nonnegative multiples of the cleared difference degree, with the exceptional canonical correction. |
| q-kernel-base-cohomology-vanishing | H^p\bigl(Y,R^q f_*(K_V+D_V)\otimes Q^k\bigr) | = | $0$ | Positive base cohomological degree and nonnegative direct-image and twist degrees. |
| q-curvature-null-base-dimension | \dim Y | ≤ | $r$ | The remaining intermediate-curvature-rank case of ordinary conversion. |
| q-alteration-descended-degree | \widehat b | = | $b\ell^3$ | The selected Galois extension degree and the relative section degree; the powers are not asserted to be minimal. |
| q-eventual-boundary-pole-order | u_s(B) | = | $\left\lfloor\min_i\frac{e_i+sc_i}{b_i}\right\rfloor$ | Divisorial pullback multiplicities, Jacobian orders and rational-section orders at a fixed base prime. |
| q-zero-degree-invariant-section-count | h^0(Y,K_Y+A_0) | = | $1$ | The natural anticanonical linearization and the zero exponent. |
| q-holonomy-tensor-vanishing | H^0\bigl(P,(\Omega_P^1)^{\otimes m}\bigr) | = | $0$ | Every positive covariant tensor degree. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-equidimensional-model** [claim](#stmt-val-flat-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-val-flat-model): Use the projective Hilbert family, resolve the base, normalize the integral flat total space, and apply Stein factorization for connectedness. -> A normal equidimensional projective model and a smooth resolution.
- **step-maximum-metric** [claim](#stmt-har-prop-maximum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-har-prop-maximum): Subtract normalized vertical minima, apply smooth adjoint direct-image positivity, take locally uniform moment limits, and exclude zeros of the maximum by extension and the maximum principle. -> A regular base-line injection and a positive continuous semipositive maximum metric.
- **step-character-sensitive-transfer** [claim](#stmt-inj-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-har-prop-maximum-2): Construct the common invariant ratio field, normalize a relative section, prove every invariant adjoint rank, retain a fixed multiplier ideal with exceptional correction, and interpolate the Euler polynomial from zero. -> An untwisted invariant section in a positive degree.
- **step-natural-invariant-index** [claim](#stmt-har-prop-index) / [proof](#sec:cohomological-input): Import the compact-complex invariant index theorem with its actual value at zero, rather than an eventual-polynomial extrapolation. -> An index polynomial on an arithmetic progression with the prescribed constant term.
- **step-forms-and-determinant** [claim](#stmt-har-lem-forms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-har-lem-forms): Select a fixed nonzero invariant cohomological degree, apply equivariant smooth semipositive hard Lefschetz, saturate the generic exterior determinant, and wedge far-tail forms against complementary fixed basis forms. -> Invariant sections directly or an unbounded determinant fixed-error family.
- **step-natural-invariant-nonvanishing** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-main): Use connectedness of the torus and Hodge decomposition to identify the invariant index at zero, obtain pseudoeffectivity of the inverse cotangent determinant, and apply invariant transfer. -> A natural invariant section of a positive anticanonical power.
- **step-null-determinant-divisors** [claim](#stmt-har-lem-null-divisors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-har-lem-null-divisors): Choose a Kähler metric with prescribed semipositive Ricci curvature, prove the cotangent determinant degree sign by Bochner, and pass to the degenerating Kähler-class limit. -> Nonpositive determinant degree and componentwise curvature-null divisor intersections.
- **step-source-character-index** [claim](#stmt-lem-source-character) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-source-character): Use positive attracting normal weights and leading conormal jets for section bounds, approximate nef lines by ample ones, and evaluate the equivariant fixed-point formula at the source extreme. -> Source-character bounds and the extreme full-character index identity.
- **step-exact-source-characters** [claim](#stmt-inj-extremal-theorem) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inj-extremal-theorem): Produce source-weight cohomology and eigenforms, shift the determinant and anticanonical linearizations to make the inputs invariant, transfer them, and use a rational convex combination of attained section weights. -> Exact source-character attainment and a natural invariant anticanonical section.
- **step-injectivity-base-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-inj-leray) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inj-leray): Apply singular injectivity, slice by general ample divisors preserving integrability, induct on the base, and kill the remaining Leray filtration by functoriality. -> Vanishing of positive base cohomology for all adjoint higher direct images.
- **step-ordinary-iitaka-conversion** [claim](#stmt-inj-ordinary-conversion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inj-ordinary-conversion): Separate the extreme Iitaka dimensions, prove generic adjoint rigidity in the remaining case, normalize the difference section vertically, mix a strictly base-positive integrable metric, and interpolate while retaining the actual exceptional direct image. -> An ordinary positive anticanonical section.
- **step-twisted-form-conversion** [claim](#stmt-inj-forms-input) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inj-forms-input): Handle differential degree zero directly; otherwise saturate the generic determinant of twisted forms, use inverse-determinant pseudoeffectivity, and invoke ordinary fixed-error conversion. -> A nonzero section of a positive anticanonical power.
- **step-uniform-base-smoothing** [claim](#stmt-har-lem-smoothing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-har-lem-smoothing): Convolve local continuous potentials with a strict background margin, patch by regularized maxima, and let the uniform error and negative-curvature allowance tend to zero. -> Uniformly convergent smooth metrics with controlled curvature errors.
- **step-harmonic-kernel-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-har-thm-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-har-thm-kernel): Take weak limits of uniformly bounded harmonic representatives, extract base canonical wedge factors from the curvature kernel, pass the Chern equation distributionally, detect classes on general fibers, and use smooth-coefficient derived decomposition. -> Injective Leray edge restriction and positive base-degree vanishing.
- **step-domination-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-har-prop-dominationvanish) / [proof](#proof-stmt-har-prop-dominationvanish): Pair pseudoeffective domination with the closed positive form built from the limiting harmonic representative; positivity forces the base wedge factor and hence the same fiber detection. -> The harmonic vanishing conclusion without assuming kernel inclusion.
- **step-birational-euler-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-har-prop-euler) / [proof](#proof-stmt-har-prop-euler): Retain the exceptional canonical direct image or its invariant summand, identify its twisted section dimensions with its Euler polynomial including zero, and multiply by the base-line injection before birational pushforward. -> Ordinary or equivariant anticanonical nonvanishing.
- **step-invariant-iitaka-harmonic-route** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-har-prop-euler-2): Maximize a fixed invariant system, order horizontal determinant multiplicities, prove invariant adjoint rigidity including zero, and derive the kernel inclusion from null-divisor intersections. -> A harmonic diagram giving natural invariant nonvanishing.
- **step-pole-sheaf-alternative** [claim](#stmt-har-prop-pole-stabilization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-har-prop-pole-stabilization): Bound all pole sheaves by a fixed divisor, use Noetherian stabilization and harmonic regularity to prove nefness, and blow up a hypothetical zero fiber to obtain a negative exceptional degree. -> Stabilized pole sheaves, nef base line and no wholly vanishing fiber.
- **step-source-retraction-map** [claim](#stmt-har-lem-source) / [proof](#proof-stmt-har-lem-source): Use the source-character bounds, construct a multiplicative rational identification along orbits, prove regularity first for ample powers and then for nef lines by ample perturbation. -> Source weight bounds, source index and regular equivariant retraction map.
- **step-harmonic-source-weight-attainment** [claim](#stmt-har-prop-extremal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-har-prop-extremal): Extract determinant eigensections, maximize dominated systems, normalize a relative eigensection, compare the base source weight, retract to that source, and interpolate in its specified weight. -> Exact attainment of the natural source weight.
- **step-extremal-to-torus-invariants** [claim](#stmt-har-cor-torus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-har-cor-torus): Choose full torus eigenvectors attaining source weights, show the source characters have a convex hull containing zero, clear rational coefficients and section exponents, and multiply nonzero eigensections. -> A natural invariant positive anticanonical section.
- **step-curvature-null-alteration-conversion** [claim](#stmt-thm-alteration-conversion) / [proof](#har:subsec:kernelfield): Construct the relatively algebraically closed null-direction field, prove affine horizontal divisors and generic adjoint rigidity, build the multiplication Hom line on a reduced flat alteration, then descend the metric by a finite quotient and its injection by a field norm. -> A continuous semipositive base injection on birational models and an ordinary anticanonical section.
- **step-localized-ideal-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-inj-local-nadel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inj-local-nadel): Obtain jets on a strictly positive ball, remove ample twists to produce an integrable divisor metric, and mix it with the original metric using Hölder and strong openness to preserve the multiplier ideal. -> A globally strictly positive metric with the same multiplier ideal and adjoint higher-cohomology vanishing.
- **step-horizontal-direct-image-strictness** [claim](#stmt-inj-strict-direct-image) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inj-strict-direct-image): Choose a fiber adjoint representative adapted to the curvature formula; integrate the positive horizontal contribution on the nonvanishing patch and preserve it under the orthogonal character splitting. -> Strict direct-image curvature in every nonzero base direction.
- **step-exact-adjoint-ideal** [claim](#stmt-inj-exact-adjoint-ideal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inj-exact-adjoint-ideal): Determine the reflexive hull by divisorial order tests, extend the invariant fiber-integral metric, and use Fubini plus extension of integrable meromorphic top forms to identify its ideal with the actual direct image. -> Exact direct-image multiplier-ideal identity, with strictness on a base ball under the horizontal patch condition.
- **step-degree-zero-field-route** [claim](#stmt-inj-degree-zero-route) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inj-degree-zero-route): Construct the invariant degree-zero pole field, normalize the relative section, prove generic adjoint rigidity and a horizontal curvature patch, retain the exact degree-zero ideal, and interpolate its Euler polynomial. -> A natural invariant positive anticanonical section.
- **step-stabilized-actual-lines** [claim](#stmt-inj-stabilized-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inj-stabilized-transfer): Prove generic invariant adjoint rigidity, obtain actual reflexive lines on a birational toroidal model, stabilize the minima of affine boundary orders, retain the zero-degree line through the intercept inclusion, and use integrable quotient metrics and strong openness for interpolation. -> A natural invariant section in positive untwisted degree.
- **step-covariant-tensor-holonomy** [claim](#stmt-prop-tensor-holonomy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tensor-holonomy): Use the Bochner identity to make holomorphic covariant tensors parallel, analyze the determinant character of connected irreducible holonomy, and apply the central scalar action to exclude invariant tensors in the nontrivial-character branch. -> A parallel canonical volume form or vanishing of every positive covariant tensor power.
- **step-compact-monodromy-section-descent** [claim](#stmt-thm-ordinary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inj-stabilized-transfer-2): Use the compact-monodromy universal-cover splitting, algebraize the compact torus action on the factor without forms, tensor an invariant anticanonical section with parallel frames, descend to the finite cover, and take the finite étale norm. -> A nonzero ordinary positive anticanonical section on the original variety.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-character-preserving-transfer**: An unbounded family of invariant sections with a fixed error whose inverse is pseudoeffective yields an invariant section in a positive untwisted degree, preserving the chosen anticanonical linearization.
- **invariant-actual-index-at-zero**: The invariant anticanonical index agrees with a polynomial along the specified positive degrees, and its value at zero is the actual invariant structure-sheaf index.
- **invariant-maximum-metric-normalization**: Divisorial normalization of the relative section produces a regular injection from a base line whose fiber-maximum norms define a positive continuous semipositive metric.
- **invariant-kernel-condition-vanishing**: Higher base cohomology of all the indicated higher direct images vanishes when curvature-null directions are vertical on a nonempty ordinary open subset and the base twist has a continuous semipositive metric.
- **invariant-exact-direct-image-ideal**: The extended invariant adjoint metric recovers the actual rank-one direct image through its multiplier ideal, retaining the defect from the reflexive hull.
- **invariant-positive-power-ordinary-nonvanishing**: Every smooth connected projective complex variety with a smoothly semipositive anticanonical bundle has a nonzero section of some positive anticanonical power.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-numerical-nefness-as-smooth-curvature**: Apply the transfer and harmonic conclusions to a merely nef anticanonical bundle. -> Numerical positivity does not establish the smooth curvature identities or the curvature-kernel condition required by these proofs.
- **anti-use-finite-sample-as-unbounded-input**: Treat a finite collection of invariant twisted sections as sufficient transfer input. -> A finite sample does not establish the unbounded family used to force effective slopes and construct the relative section.
- **anti-use-shifted-linearization-in-natural-index**: Use the invariant anticanonical index to supply cohomological input for an arbitrary shifted linearization. -> The index premise specifies the natural linearization; changing the character changes the invariant spaces.
- **anti-use-arbitrary-positive-direct-image-subline**: Infer semipositivity for an arbitrary subline of a positive direct image. -> The invariant metric argument requires a holomorphic orthogonal quotient; arbitrary subline positivity is not supplied.
- **anti-use-continuous-upstairs-decomposition**: Apply the derived decomposition directly to a coefficient upstairs with only a continuous metric. -> The decomposition used in the harmonic proof applies to the smooth coefficient; the continuous line is tensorized from the base afterwards.
- **anti-use-reflexive-hull-in-place-of-actual-image**: Replace the actual rank-one direct image by its reflexive hull and discard the multiplier ideal. -> This removes possible conditions in codimension two and loses the integrability information needed for interpolation.
- **anti-use-uniform-or-prescribed-exponent**: Read ordinary nonvanishing as a guarantee at a prescribed exponent or as a uniform bound on the required degree. -> The conclusion gives existence in some positive degree, and descent can increase that degree without supplying a uniform exponent.
- **anti-use-alteration-as-birational-euler-source**: Apply birational Euler interpolation directly on the generically finite alteration. -> The interpolation proposition requires a source birational to the original variety; the alteration construction must first descend its line and injection.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper proves existence of sections on smooth projective complex varieties through unbounded section families, singular metric integrability, harmonic weak limits, vanishing, and Euler interpolation. It supplies no finite input representation or effective procedure producing the required exponent and section. The descent discussion explicitly states: “None of these arguments supplies a uniform exponent.” Finite checks of boundary-order arithmetic would cover an auxiliary calculation only and would not decide the geometric nonvanishing or analytic hypotheses.

It provides no relation to accelerator capacity, memory consumption, or throughput. |

It requires smooth curvature and a verified vertical-kernel inclusion, neither of which is supplied by a discrete network of agents or replicas. |

This is a mathematical construction from established geometric data; the existence proof supplies no effective general section finder or uniform exponent. |

Give $L=-K_X$ its natural linearization and a smooth semipositive metric invariant under the maximal compact torus. Put $L'=\\pi^*L$, $J=K_{V/X}$, and let $t$ be its natural invariant Jacobian section. Assume that $$\\mathcal B=(f_*\\mathcal O_V(J))^T$$ has rank one. Via division by $t$, view $\\mathcal B$ as a sheaf of rational functions on $Y$, and write $\\mathcal B^{\\vee\\vee}=\\mathcal O_Y(B_0)$ and $R=B_0-K_Y$. Then the invariant adjoint $L^2$ metric extends to a singular semipositive metric $\\psi_R$ on $R$, and $$\\label{inj:exact-ideal}\n \\mathcal O_Y(B_0)\\otimes\\mathcal I(\\psi_R)=\\mathcal B.$$ If, on a nonempty patch in the smooth base-change locus where $t$ is nonzero, the curvature of $L'$ dominates the pullback of a positive base form, then $\\psi_R$ is smooth and strictly positive on a base coordinate ball.",
          "source_object": "Exact ideal of an invariant adjoint line"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-smooth-projective-complex-scope** (hypothesis): Ordinary anticanonical nonvanishing is stated for smooth connected projective complex varieties. Its conclusion does not establish corresponding results for singular varieties, positive characteristic, or arbitrary nonprojective complex spaces. Blocked use: ['Apply ordinary nonvanishing outside the stated smooth projective complex setting.']
- **limit-smooth-metric-versus-nefness** (hypothesis): The transfer and geometric conversion arguments require an actual smooth semipositive anticanonical metric. Numerical nefness alone does not establish the curvature input. Blocked use: ['Replace smooth Hermitian semipositivity by numerical nefness when invoking transfer or harmonic restriction.']
- **limit-fixed-pseudoeffective-inverse-error** (hypothesis): Invariant transfer requires a fixed linearized error whose inverse is pseudoeffective, together with a chosen line ordinarily equal to the anticanonical bundle. Blocked use: ['Use varying error bundles or an error of the opposite pseudoeffective sign as transfer input.', 'Apply the anticanonical transfer conclusion to an unrelated ordinary line bundle.']
- **limit-unbounded-input-not-finite-sampling** (hypothesis): The input section family must exist in unbounded positive degrees. A finite list of computed sections does not establish that premise. Blocked use: ['Infer fixed-error transfer from a finite sample of invariant twisted sections.']
- **limit-natural-index-versus-shifted-transfer** (hypothesis): The invariant index uses the natural anticanonical linearization. Permission to shift the linearization in transfer does not extend the index statement to arbitrary character shifts. Blocked use: ['Obtain shifted invariant cohomological input by applying the natural invariant index without a separate justification.']
- **limit-invariant-euler-premise** (hypothesis): Natural invariant nonvanishing from cohomological input requires a nonzero structure-sheaf Euler characteristic. Ordinary nonvanishing without that premise uses compact-monodromy descent and does not automatically give invariance for an arbitrary action on the original variety. Blocked use: ['Remove the Euler premise from the natural invariant corollary solely by citing the ordinary nonvanishing conclusion.']
- **limit-normal-model-versus-smooth-resolution** (formal_scope): Equidimensionality belongs to the normal model used for divisorial normalization. A smooth resolution is used for fiber integrals and need not remain equidimensional. Blocked use: ['Assume every vertical prime on an arbitrary smooth resolution dominates a base prime.', 'Perform vertical-minimum normalization using an unverified equidimensionality assertion upstairs.']
- **limit-connected-fibers-and-generic-ranks** (hypothesis): The connected-fiber assertion in the model lemma requires the stated function-field condition. Maximum-metric construction requires the indicated generic adjoint spaces in every relevant degree, including the zero exponent. Blocked use: ['Assume connected fibers merely from dominance.', 'Use an eventual positive-degree rank computation in place of the full generic adjoint condition.']
- **limit-generic-rank-not-singular-fiber-rank** (formal_scope): A rank computation on a dense base-change open does not assert the same section dimension on singular fibers. Blocked use: ['Promote generic adjoint rigidity to a constant-rank assertion on every boundary fiber.']
- **limit-relative-section-boundary-regularity** (hypothesis): Regularity on the generic fiber does not remove boundary poles. The normalized relative section must pass the horizontal and vertical divisorial regularity tests on the normal model. Blocked use: ['Push a generic-fiber section directly to the original variety without controlling boundary poles.']
- **limit-direct-image-quotient-positivity** (formal_scope): Positivity of the invariant adjoint line is justified through the specified holomorphic quotient. It is not a positivity theorem for arbitrary singular subbundles. Blocked use: ['Infer semipositivity for an arbitrary adjoint subline from positivity of the ambient direct image.']
- **limit-fixed-ideal-not-trivial-ideal** (formal_scope): Character-sensitive interpolation retains a fixed multiplier ideal that need not be trivial. Bounded semipositive twists preserve that ideal rather than eliminate it. Blocked use: ['Discard the multiplier ideal from the corrected base sheaf.', 'Replace its exceptional-support inclusion by an unsupported claim that the ideal is trivial.']
- **limit-injectivity-trivial-ideal-and-domination** (hypothesis): The singular-injectivity route requires both a trivial coefficient multiplier ideal and strict domination of a positive ample base curvature form. Blocked use: ['Invoke adjoint higher-direct-image vanishing with an arbitrary singular coefficient metric lacking either condition.']
- **limit-harmonic-smooth-coefficient-continuous-base** (hypothesis): Harmonic decomposition applies to the smooth semipositive coefficient on the total space. Continuity enters through a line pulled back from the base and requires uniform smoothing in the stated argument. Blocked use: ['Apply the smooth-coefficient decomposition theorem directly to a merely continuous coefficient upstairs.', 'Substitute locally bounded discontinuous base weights for the continuous metric required by the uniform approximation proof.']
- **limit-kernel-or-domination-must-be-proved** (hypothesis): Harmonic vanishing requires curvature-null directions to be vertical on a nonempty ordinary submersion open, or the separately stated pseudoeffective domination alternative. Blocked use: ['Infer the vertical-kernel condition from nefness.', 'Invoke the domination alternative without an ample base line and the required pseudoeffective difference.']
- **limit-canonical-valued-fiber-restriction** (formal_scope): The harmonic restriction is restriction of a coefficient-valued Dolbeault class with its canonical base factor retained. It is not ordinary pullback of an absolute top-degree form to a lower-dimensional fiber. Blocked use: ['Replace canonical-valued cohomological restriction by ordinary differential-form pullback and infer that the restriction vanishes.']
- **limit-birational-euler-source** (hypothesis): Euler interpolation on the exceptional canonical direct image requires a source birational to the original variety. A genuinely generically finite alteration must first descend its metrized line and injection to birational models. Blocked use: ['Apply birational Euler interpolation directly to the altered source.']
- **limit-reduced-fibers-in-hom-line-construction** (hypothesis): In the Hom-line alteration construction, reduced fibers are needed to deduce a nonzero pointwise value from a nonzero map on a fiber. Blocked use: ['Infer positivity of the fiberwise maximum from a nonzero fiber map on a possibly nonreduced fiber.']
- **limit-exact-ideal-retains-sheaf-defect** (formal_scope): The exact adjoint multiplier ideal retains the difference between the actual direct image and its reflexive hull, including conditions in higher codimension. Blocked use: ['Replace the actual adjoint direct image by its reflexive hull while discarding its multiplier ideal.']
- **limit-local-strictness-requires-nonvanishing-patch** (hypothesis): Strict direct-image curvature requires horizontal curvature domination on a patch where the specified fiber section is nonzero. Localized multiplier-ideal vanishing also requires continuous weights and strict positivity on a coordinate ball. Blocked use: ['Deduce strict direct-image curvature from positivity on a patch where the chosen section vanishes.', 'Invoke localized vanishing from global semipositivity alone.']
- **limit-stabilized-lines-actual-frames-and-intercept** (formal_scope): Stabilized-order interpolation uses actual direct-image frames with holomorphic lifts and the inclusion comparing the eventual family with the actual zero-degree line. Eventual affine orders alone do not retain the required constant term. Blocked use: ['Use a reflexive-hull frame without a holomorphic lift to assert integrability.', 'Replace the actual zero-degree line by the eventual intercept without proving the section-preserving inclusion.']
- **limit-source-character-hypotheses** (hypothesis): Exact source-character attainment requires the stated vanishing of positive-degree holomorphic forms and a linearized ample bundle. The separate harmonic source-weight route assumes rational connectedness. Blocked use: ['Apply source-character attainment to a variety with uncontrolled holomorphic forms.', 'Remove rational connectedness from the harmonic source-weight proposition without supplying a different argument.']
- **limit-descent-monodromy-and-etale-norm** (hypothesis): The finite cover need not be a product. Section descent requires invariance under the retained deck action, including the compact-torus action on the compact factor and the canonical coefficient frames. The final norm uses finite étale descent. Blocked use: ['Treat the finite cover as a product without justification.', 'Descend an arbitrary non-invariant section through retained monodromy.', 'Use canonical pullback identities for a ramified cover as though it were étale.']
- **limit-ordinary-form-conversion-not-equivariant** (formal_scope): Twisted-form conversion produces ordinary sections on the smooth projective cover after the forms have descended there. It does not assert equivariance of the conversion. Blocked use: ['Claim character preservation from the ordinary twisted-form conversion.', 'Apply that conversion on the compact factor and assume arbitrary resulting sections descend through monodromy.']
- **limit-existential-degree-no-uniform-bound** (nonconstructive): The conclusion gives a section in some positive power. It supplies neither a prescribed exponent nor a uniform bound; common powers and finite norms can increase the exponent. Blocked use: ['Assert a section of the anticanonical bundle itself from positive-power nonvanishing.', 'Claim a uniformly bounded stopping degree for a general section search.']
- **limit-finite-testing-does-not-certify-geometric-existence** (nonconstructive): Finite arithmetic checks of boundary orders cannot certify the unbounded section hypothesis, analytic metric constructions, or global geometric nonvanishing. An effective general finite computation contract is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Present finite auxiliary arithmetic agreement as certification of the full transfer or ordinary nonvanishing theorem.']
- **limit-holonomy-dichotomy-factor-scope** (hypothesis): The tensor-holonomy dichotomy requires a compact simply connected nonflat Kähler factor that is irreducible in the Riemannian de Rham sense and has nonnegative Ricci curvature. Tensor vanishing is only the alternative to a parallel canonical volume form. Blocked use: ['Apply the dichotomy to arbitrary reducible or non-simply-connected spaces.', 'Assert tensor vanishing in the parallel-volume-form branch.']

The normalization $V$ of $X_0$ is equidimensional over $Y'$; in particular, every vertical prime divisor of $V$ maps onto a prime divisor of $Y'$. If $\\mathbb C(Y)$ is algebraically closed in $\\mathbb C(X)$, the morphism from $V$, and from any smooth resolution of $V$, to $Y'$ has connected fibers. Suppose an algebraic torus $T$ acts equivariantly on these maps and trivially on $Y$. Give $L=-K_X$ its natural linearization and a smooth semipositive metric invariant under the maximal compact torus. Put $L'=\\pi^*L$, $J=K_{V/X}$, and let $t$ be its natural invariant Jacobian section. Assume that $$\\mathcal B=(f_*\\mathcal O_V(J))^T$$ has rank one. Via division by $t$, view $\\mathcal B$ as a sheaf of rational functions on $Y$, and write $\\mathcal B^{\\vee\\vee}=\\mathcal O_Y(B_0)$ and $R=B_0-K_Y$. Then the invariant adjoint $L^2$ metric extends to a singular semipositive metric $\\psi_R$ on $R$, and $$\\label{inj:exact-ideal}\n \\mathcal O_Y(B_0)\\otimes\\mathcal I(\\psi_R)=\\mathcal B.$$ If, on a nonempty patch in the smooth base-change locus where $t$ is nonzero, the curvature of $L'$ dominates the pullback of a positive base form, then $\\psi_R$ is smooth and strictly positive on a base coordinate ball.",

        }

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "sec:descent",
          "exact_quote": "### Compact monodromy and the descent maps\n\n<span id=\"har:sec:descent\" label=\"har:sec:descent\"></span> We prove Corollary <a href=\"#thm:ordinary\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:ordinary\">2</a> by applying invariant nonvanishing to the compact factor in the universal cover and then taking a finite norm. We use the structure statement of (OpenAI 2026a, Theorem <span class=\"nodecor\">(Finite cover with compact torus monodromy)</span>) [[CompanionH](#ref-CompanionH)], not that companion’s nonvanishing conclusion. For a smooth connected projective $X$ with smoothly semipositive $-K_X$, it gives a holomorphic isometric universal-cover splitting $$\\widetilde X=\\mathbb C^c\\times V\\times F,$$ where $V$ is a product of compact simply connected Ricci-flat factors with invariant parallel canonical frames and $F$ is a product of compact projective factors with no positive-degree holomorphic forms. After finite index, the deck subgroup acts faithfully by a translation lattice on $\\mathbb C^c$, trivially on $V$, and through a compact real torus $H$ on $F$. Empty products are points. The associated finite cover $X'$ is smooth projective and need not be a product. These are the only geometric conclusions from the companion that we need.\n\nThe compact factor $F$ has $H^j(F,\\mathcal O_F)=0$ for $j>0$ by Hodge symmetry, and its anticanonical metric is smoothly semipositive. We first descend an invariant section. Afterwards we construct two additional descent maps, preserving a cohomology class or a twisted form, to which the ordinary conversions apply on $X'$.",
          "source_id": "PM-068.4"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-inj-stabilized-transfer-2",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-inj-stabilized-transfer</summary>\n\n*Proof of Corollary <a href=\"#thm:ordinary\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:ordinary\">2</a>.* On $F$, the compact torus preserves a very ample line because it preserves its Chern class and $\\operatorname{Pic}^0(F)=0$. Its projective action has algebraic-torus Zariski closure: a compact connected abelian subgroup of a projective linear group is conjugate into a diagonal compact torus. Passing through the finite special-linear cover, if necessary, provides a linearization of a very ample power. Invariants of the natural anticanonical line for the compact group and its Zariski closure agree. Corollary <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">7</a> therefore gives a nonzero invariant section of $-mK_F$ for some $m>0$.\n\nTensor its pullback to $\\widetilde X$ with the $m$-th powers of the inverse canonical frames on $\\mathbb C^c$ and $V$. This nonzero product is invariant under the selected deck subgroup and descends to a section on $X'$. If $F$ is a point, take its section to be $1$ and $m=1$.\n\nThe finite étale norm (OpenAI 2026a, Lemma <span class=\"nodecor\">(Finite étale norm)</span>) [[CompanionH](#ref-CompanionH)] sends this section to a nonzero section of a positive power of $-K_X$. Explicitly, pull to a connected finite Galois refinement, multiply all Galois translates, and descend their invariant product. The product is nonzero on the integral cover, and canonical bundles pull back under étale morphisms. Projectivity and GAGA make the resulting holomorphic section algebraic. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-068.4"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-descent-monodromy-and-etale-norm",

          "exact_quote": "#### Descending twisted forms before Iitaka conversion\n\nThere is a direct twisted-form route to the ordinary conversion of Section <a href=\"#inj:injectivity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"inj:injectivity\">6</a>. Keep the product $\\widetilde X=\\mathbb C^c\\times V\\times F$ and the finite-index deck subgroup just constructed, whose action is by translations on $\\mathbb C^c$, trivial on $V$, and contained in the compact torus $H$ on $F$. Put $P=-K_F$ and $d=\\dim F$. The invariant index has constant term one. Hence for a fixed $q$ and unbounded positive integers $m$ there are nonzero invariant classes in $$H^q(F,mP)^H=H^q(F,K_F+(m+1)P)^H.$$ Choose an $H$-invariant Kähler form. Smooth semipositive hard Lefschetz (Demailly, Peternell, and Schneider 2001) [[DPS2001](#ref-DPS2001)] is an equivariant surjection from $H^0(F,\\Omega_F^{d-q}\\otimes (m+1)P)$ onto the displayed group. Averaging a preimage over $H$ keeps its nonzero image, so supplies $$0\\ne\\alpha_m\\in\n H^0(F,\\Omega_F^{d-q}\\otimes (m+1)P)^H.$$ Set $p=d-q$. Let $\\tau_0$ be the translation-invariant inverse canonical frame on $\\mathbb C^c$, and let $\\tau_V$ be a product of the parallel inverse canonical frames on $V$. Projection to $F$ and the natural inclusion of its pulled-back cotangent bundle into the product cotangent bundle give the holomorphic twisted form $$\\widetilde\\alpha_m=\n \\operatorname{pr}_F^*\\alpha_m\\otimes\n (\\tau_0\\otimes\\tau_V)^{m+1}\n \\in H^0\\bigl(\\widetilde X,\n       \\Omega_{\\widetilde X}^{p}\\otimes(-K_{\\widetilde X})^{m+1}\\bigr).$$ It is nonzero: evaluate $\\alpha_m$ on a tangent $p$-tuple at a point where it is nonzero and use the same tuple in the product; the other coefficient frames never vanish. The $m+1$ coefficient exponent matches the full anticanonical line of the product.\n\nEvery factor in this expression is invariant under the selected deck subgroup. The resulting form therefore descends through the covering map to a holomorphic section $$0\\ne\\beta_m\\in\n H^0\\bigl(X',\\Omega_{X'}^{p}\\otimes(-K_{X'})^{m+1}\\bigr)$$ on the associated smooth projective finite étale cover $X'$ of $X$. Descent follows on evenly covered coordinate neighborhoods, where invariant local forms glue; its pullback is $\\widetilde\\alpha_m$, so the descended form cannot be zero. For every fixed $r>0$ the same construction is linear and gives the injection $$H^0(F,\\Omega_F^p\\otimes rP)^H\n \\lhook\\joinrel\\longrightarrow\n H^0(X',\\Omega_{X'}^p\\otimes(-rK_{X'})).$$ Projectivity and GAGA make $\\beta_m$ algebraic. If $F$ is a point, take $p=q=0$ and $\\alpha_m=1$; the same construction applies.\n\nNow apply Corollary <a href=\"#inj:forms-input\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"inj:forms-input\">14</a> on $X'$ itself to this fixed differential degree and the unbounded positive twists $m+1$. It gives a nonzero positive anticanonical section on $X'$. Pull back to a finite connected Galois refinement and multiply its translates, as in Section <a href=\"#har:subsec:sectiondescent\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"har:subsec:sectiondescent\">14.1</a>, to obtain a nonzero positive anticanonical section on $X$. Thus this route descends the twisted forms before using the ordinary Iitaka and injectivity conversion; the cohomology and alteration route of the preceding subsection remains a separate construction.",

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Let <i>X</i> be a smooth projective complex variety with smoothly semipositive anticanonical bundle. For any torus linearization of that bundle, we show that invariant sections in unbounded degrees with one fixed negative pseudoeffective error yield an invariant section in a positive untwisted degree. Combining the natural invariant index with compact-monodromy structure, we deduce that every such <i>X</i> has a nonzero section of some positive anticanonical power.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective variety over $\mathbb C$ and put $L=-K_X=\det T_X$. Anticanonical nonvanishing asks whether $H^0(X,mL)\ne0$ for some integer $m>0$. We assume that $L$ is smoothly semipositive: it has a smooth Hermitian metric with nonnegative Chern curvature. Cohomological constructions can produce sections with a fixed twist, which must then be removed. In the presence of a group action there is a second obstruction: the resulting section must have the required character. Our principal theorem addresses these two obstructions together.

Let an algebraic torus $T$ act on $X$. A $T$-linearization of a line bundle is a lift of this action to its total space, linear on each fiber. The tangent action gives the natural linearization of $L$, but tensoring that action by a character of $T$ gives another one. We write $P$ for $L$ equipped with any such linearization, and use additive notation for tensor products. Thus $H^0(X,mP)^T$ specifies both a degree and a character of an anticanonical section.

The input to our principal theorem is a sequence of sections of $M+iP$ with a fixed error $M$. The assumption on the error is that $-M$ is pseudoeffective: its numerical class lies in the closure of the effective cone.

<a id="stmt-inj-transfer"></a>
<div id="inj:transfer" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Invariant transfer). *Let $X$ be smooth connected projective, let $L=-K_X$ carry a smooth semipositive metric, and let an algebraic torus $T$ act on $X$. Let $P,M$ be linearized line bundles such that $P=L$ as ordinary line bundles and $-M$ is pseudoeffective. The linearization of $P$ may differ from the natural one on $L$. Suppose that for an unbounded $I\subset\mathbb Z_{>0}$ there are nonzero invariant sections $$s_i\in H^0(X,M+iP)^T\qquad(i\in I).$$ Then $H^0(X,mP)^T\ne0$ for some integer $m>0$.*

</div>

The theorem removes the fixed error without losing the specified character. It assumes neither vanishing of $H^1(X,\mathcal O_X)$ nor a condition on the Euler characteristic. Taking the trivial torus gives ordinary fixed-error conversion. With the natural linearization, invariant cohomology supplies an important source of the required twisted sections. In Section <a href="#sec:cohomological-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cohomological-input">4</a> we deduce that $$\chi(X,\mathcal O_X)\ne0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 H^0(X,-mK_X)^T\ne0\text{ for some }m>0.$$ The integer may depend on $X$. The distinction between natural and shifted linearizations remains essential in this deduction: the invariant anticanonical index is applied only with the natural one.

Combining this natural invariant nonvanishing with the compact-monodromy structure theorem of (OpenAI 2026a, Theorem <span class="nodecor">(Finite cover with compact torus monodromy)</span>) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[CompanionH](#ref-CompanionH)] removes the Euler-characteristic premise from the ordinary conclusion. The structure theorem separates the universal cover into a Euclidean factor, compact factors with parallel canonical frames, and a compact factor with no positive-degree holomorphic forms. After a finite cover, the remaining action on the last factor lies in a compact torus. Its invariant section therefore descends, and a finite norm returns a section to $X$. Section <a href="#sec:descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:descent">14</a> proves the following consequence and constructs two additional descent maps, for cohomology and for twisted forms.

<a id="stmt-thm-ordinary"></a>
<div id="thm:ordinary" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *For every smooth connected projective complex $X$ with smoothly semipositive $-K_X$, some positive multiple $-mK_X$ has a nonzero section.*

</div>

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Why a fibration removes the error

Choose nonzero invariant input sections $s_i$. Ratios of products having the same linearized degree are invariant rational functions. Their common field determines a rational map from $X$ to a projective base. Along its generic fiber, the divisors of the $s_i$ vary affinely with $i$. Unboundedness forces their slope to be effective, so a quotient $s_j/s_i$ supplies a relative anticanonical section.

The main difficulty is to extend this relative section across the boundary. We work with a birational morphism $\mu:W\to X$ and a normal equidimensional model $f_W:W\to Y$, and put $P_W=\mu^*P$. Every vertical prime of $W$ then lies over a prime of $Y$. Subtracting the smallest normalized order over each base prime gives a regular section of $kP_W-f_W^*(kB)$, where $B$ is a rational divisor on $Y$ and $kB$ is integral. On a smooth resolution $\pi:V\to X$ through $W$, put $E=K_{V/X}$. The same ratio field shows that the invariant sections of $E+jk\pi^*P$ on the generic fiber of $V\to Y$ form a one-dimensional space over $\mathbb C(Y)$ for every $j\ge0$, including zero.

Two metrics now play different roles. Smooth adjoint fiber integrals in increasing degrees converge, after taking roots, to a fiberwise maximum. This gives a bounded semipositive metric on $kB$. At degree zero, pseudoeffectivity of $-M$ supplies a different metric with positive curvature in the base directions. Its fiber integral extends to $-K_Y+D_*$, where $D_*\ge0$ pulls back to an exceptional divisor over $X$. Its multiplier ideal $\mathcal J$ can be nontrivial. What matters is the inclusion $\mathcal O_Y(-D_*)\subseteq\mathcal J$ and the fact that tensoring by the bounded metric on $jkB$ leaves $\mathcal J$ unchanged.

Nadel vanishing therefore turns the section dimension of $\mathcal O_Y(D_*+jkB)\otimes\mathcal J$ into a polynomial in $j$, including at zero. Its value there is positive, so it cannot vanish at every positive integer. A resulting section lifts by the relative section, and the exceptional correction disappears on $X$. Section <a href="#sec:models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:models">2</a> prepares the model and the maximum metric; Section <a href="#inj:character-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inj:character-transfer">3</a> gives the complete transfer proof.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### A separate harmonic mechanism

There is another way to detect the zero exponent, using restriction of cohomology to fibers. Its hypotheses and purpose differ from character-sensitive transfer. Let $f:V\to Y$ be a surjective morphism between smooth projective varieties, let $D_V$ have a smooth semipositive metric of curvature $\theta$, and let $Q$ have a continuous semipositive metric on $Y$. Suppose that on a nonempty ordinary open set where $f$ is a submersion, every null direction of $\theta$ is vertical. We prove in Theorem <a href="#har:thm:kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="har:thm:kernel">16</a> that $$H^p\bigl(Y,R^qf_*(K_V+D_V)\otimes Q^k\bigr)=0
 \qquad(p>0,\ q,k\ge0).$$ The proof approximates only the continuous metric pulled back from $Y$. A weak limit of harmonic representatives has all base canonical covectors as wedge factors, and its relative part detects a nonzero cohomology class on a general fiber. We apply decomposition to the smooth coefficient $D_V$ and then tensor the resulting derived isomorphism by $Q^k$. Thus the argument uses no decomposition theorem for a merely continuous coefficient upstairs.

We work through the natural-linearization application completely, then explain alternative constructions at the point where their geometric input changes. A fixed invariant system, null-pole functions, and determinant-semigroup ratios give different ways to choose the base. An ordinary curvature-null field leads instead to a reduced flat alteration, a line of multiplication maps, and a norm back to the original function field. Local strictness with an exact direct-image ideal and stabilization of actual direct-image lines provide two further vanishing mechanisms. Each construction is carried through to its section conclusion; the common analytic conclusions are reused only after their hypotheses have been verified.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Context and analytic predecessors

Anticanonical nonvanishing is motivated by the questions recorded by Yau (Yau 1994, Problem 75) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Yau1994](#ref-Yau1994)]. Our conclusions concern a positive power, whose exponent may vary with the variety. Smooth semipositivity provides curvature identities unavailable from numerical nefness alone. Demailly–Peternell–Schneider established the universal-cover splitting and finite-cover Albanese fibration for a Hermitian semipositive anticanonical bundle (Demailly, Peternell, and Schneider 1996, Structure Theorem) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[DPS1996](#ref-DPS1996)]; Campana–Demailly–Peternell identified the remaining compact factors as rationally connected (Campana, Demailly, and Peternell 2015, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[CDP2015](#ref-CDP2015)]. The finite cover is a fiber bundle, so descent must still account for monodromy. We use the precise compact-torus structure and finite étale norm in (OpenAI 2026a) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[CompanionH](#ref-CompanionH)], and construct the cohomology-valued and form-valued descent maps here.

In the broader nef setting, Lazić–Matsumura–Peternell–Tsakanikas–Xie proved numerical effectivity of $-(K_X+\Delta)$ for projective log canonical threefold pairs with nef $-(K_X+\Delta)$, assuming that $X$ is $\mathbb Q$-factorial or has rational singularities (Lazić et al. 2023, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[LMPTX2023](#ref-LMPTX2023)]. Numerical effectivity means that the numerical class is represented by an effective $\mathbb R$-divisor; it does not by itself provide a section of the specified line bundle.

Müller proves nonvanishing for projective klt pairs with nef log anticanonical divisor whose restriction to a general fiber of the maximal rationally connected fibration is semiample, and for every projective klt threefold pair with nef log anticanonical divisor (Müller 2025, Theorem A and Corollary B) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Muller2025](#ref-Muller2025)]. His equivariant theorem gives natural invariant sections for semiample log anticanonical divisors on projective sub-log canonical pairs under commutative linear algebraic groups (Müller 2025, Theorem C) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Muller2025](#ref-Muller2025)]. Here we work with smooth projective varieties and algebraic tori. Smooth semipositivity and a nonzero Euler characteristic give natural invariant sections, while Theorem <a href="#inj:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inj:transfer">1</a> allows shifted linearizations with fixed-error input. The compact-monodromy descent then gives ordinary nonvanishing without the Euler-characteristic assumption.

The independent compact-complex index theorem, with its actual value at zero, is supplied by (OpenAI 2026b, Invariant anticanonical index theorem) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[CompanionI](#ref-CompanionI)]. The passage from cohomology to twisted forms uses smooth semipositive hard Lefschetz, due to Mourougane (Mourougane 1999, Theorem 2.6 and Serre duality) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Mourougane1999](#ref-Mourougane1999)]; we use the formulation (Demailly, Peternell, and Schneider 2001, Corollary 2.1.2) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[DPS2001](#ref-DPS2001)]. The determinant construction follows Lazić–Peternell (Lazić and Peternell 2018, Lemma 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[LP2018](#ref-LP2018)] and its later use in (Lazić et al. 2023, Lemma 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[LMPTX2023](#ref-LMPTX2023)]; we prove the unbounded-twist argument in the form needed here, including the numerically trivial case. The inverse determinant is pseudoeffective by the cotangent-tensor theorem of Lazić–Matsumura–Peternell–Tsakanikas–Xie (Lazić et al. 2023, Theorem 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[LMPTX2023](#ref-LMPTX2023)], which develops Ou’s generic-nefness argument (Ou 2023, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Ou2023](#ref-Ou2023)].

Berndtsson’s smooth direct-image theorem (Berndtsson 2009) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Berndtsson2009](#ref-Berndtsson2009)] gives positivity of the fiberwise adjoint metrics. We obtain the continuous maximum metric by taking their normalized moment limits, and derive local horizontal strictness from his curvature formula. For singular coefficients, Berndtsson–Păun proved direct-image positivity over smooth families (Berndtsson and Păun 2008, Theorem 3.5) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[BP2008](#ref-BP2008)]; we use Păun–Takayama’s multiplier-ideal formulation and extension to the actual torsion-free direct image (Păun and Takayama 2018, Theorems 3.3.4–3.3.5) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[PT2018](#ref-PT2018)]. Positivity of an invariant line is obtained from a specified holomorphic quotient; it is never inferred for an arbitrary singular subbundle. Fujino–Matsumura injectivity (Fujino and Matsumura 2021) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[FM2016](#ref-FM2016)] and Nadel vanishing (Nadel 1990) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Nadel1990](#ref-Nadel1990)] supply the cohomological ending. Guan–Zhou strong openness (Guan and Zhou 2015) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[GuanZhou2015](#ref-GuanZhou2015)] is used to keep the relevant ideal fixed when a strict metric is mixed with another one.

The harmonic argument belongs to a different regularity regime. Richberg smoothing (Richberg 1967) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Richberg1968](#ref-Richberg1968)] provides uniform approximations of continuous base weights, and Fujisawa’s derived decomposition (Fujisawa 2016, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Fujisawa2015](#ref-Fujisawa2015)], obtained from Takegoshi’s harmonic representatives, applies to the smooth semipositive coefficient on $V$. Related vanishing theorems impose global curvature domination: Fujino (Fujino 2018, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Fujino2018](#ref-Fujino2018)] uses a smooth semipositive metric on the pullback of an ample base line, whereas Matsumura (Matsumura 2016, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Matsumura2018](#ref-Matsumura2018)] uses the pullback of a base Kähler form.
